- phase: Relapse
  notes: >-
    In a combined SIOP and AIEOP series, 37 of 237 patients (16%) relapsed after
    complete remission, at a median of 17 months from diagnosis; 35 of the 37
    relapses (95%) were metastatic, most often to the brain (13), lungs (7) and
    bone (5), and five-year event-free and overall survival after relapse were
    18% and 26%. A review describes a propensity for late relapse and attributes
    to improved intensive chemotherapy regimens a change in the clinical course,
    with late relapses now less frequent and the brain having overtaken bone as
    the most common site of relapse.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:24937667
    reference_title: "Treatment and outcome of patients with relapsed clear cell sarcoma of the kidney: a combined SIOP and AIEOP study."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: DIRECT
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Thirty-seven of 237 CCSK patients (16%) treated according to SIOP and AIEOP protocols developed a relapse. Median time from initial diagnosis to relapse was 17 months (range, 5.5 months - 6.6 years). Thirt-five out of thirty-seven relapses (95%) were metastatic; the most common sites of relapse were the brain (n=13), lungs (n=7) and bone (n=5). ... Five-year event-free survival (EFS) after relapse was 18% (95% CI: 4%-32%), and 5-year overall survival (OS) was 26% (95% CI: 10%-42%)."
    explanation: >-
      Among 237 CCSK patients treated on SIOP and AIEOP protocols, 37 (16%)
      relapsed after complete remission at the end of primary treatment, at a
      median of 17 months from diagnosis. 35 of the 37 relapses (95%) were
      metastatic, most often to the brain (13), lungs (7) and bone (5).
      Five-year event-free and overall survival after relapse, both measured
      from the date of relapse, were 18% (95% CI 4% to 32%) and 26% (95% CI 10%
      to 42%), so outcome after relapse is poor. 'Thirt-five' is the source's
      own spelling and is kept as written.
  - reference: PMID:30917048
    reference_title: "Clear Cell Sarcoma of the Kidney."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: DIRECT
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Clear cell sarcoma of the kidney is an uncommon malignant pediatric renal neoplasm that typically presents in the 2- to 3-year age group and has a propensity for aggressive behavior and late relapses. ... Improved intensive chemotherapy regimens have influenced the clinical course of the disease, with late relapses now being less frequent and the brain having overtaken bone as the most common site of relapse."
    explanation: >-
      This review of clear cell sarcoma of the kidney states that the tumour
      typically presents in the 2- to 3-year age group and has a propensity for
      aggressive behaviour and late relapses. It then attributes a change in the
      clinical course to improved intensive chemotherapy regimens, with late
      relapses now less frequent and the brain having overtaken bone as the most
      common site of relapse. Both statements are the review's synthesis of
      published clinical series, and the review gives no time threshold for a
      late relapse.
stages:
- name: Stage I
  description: >-
    On the National Wilms Tumor Study-5 (NWTS-5), 12 of the 108 staged patients
    had stage I disease, and five-year event-free survival for stage I was 100%.
    On the Children's Oncology Group (COG) studies AREN0321 and AREN03B2, 10 of
    132 patients (7.6%) were stage I, with a pooled four-year event-free
    survival of 90%. AREN0321 omitted radiotherapy for stage I disease when
    lymph nodes were sampled; none of the seven stage I patients treated without
    it had a local recurrence, and one stage I recurrence occurred in the brain.
    COG stage I rests on upfront nephrectomy and SIOP stage I on the tumour
    after preoperative chemotherapy, so a tumour that would be COG stage II or
    III if resected upfront could be SIOP stage I. The COG authors cite 39.5% of
    CCSK patients as stage I in the SIOP studies against 7.6% under COG
    staging, and suggest that stage I based on upfront nephrectomy is more
    favourable than stage I after neoadjuvant chemotherapy. On the SIOP 93-01
    and SIOP 2001 trials, 2 of 80 stage I patients received radiotherapy.
  notes: >-
    The SIOP 93-01 and SIOP 2001 report gives 42% of its 191 patients as stage
    I, and its radiotherapy figures count 80 stage I patients, whereas the COG
    report gives 39.5% (75 of 191) for the same studies. Neither cited text
    explains the difference.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:30255545
    reference_title: "Impact of cyclophosphamide and etoposide on outcome of clear cell sarcoma of the kidney treated on the National Wilms Tumor Study-5 (NWTS-5)."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: DIRECT
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Stage distribution was as follows: stage I, 12; II, 44; III, 45; IV, 7. ... Five-year EFS for stage I-IV was 100%, 88%, 73%, and 29%, respectively."
    explanation: >-
      The NWTS-5 abstract gives the stage distribution of the trial's 108
      eligible patients (12, 44, 45 and 7 for stages I to IV) and a five-year
      event-free survival of 100% for stage I. The figure rests on 12 patients,
      all of whom also received radiation to the tumour bed on this protocol.
  - reference: PMID:38396300
    reference_title: "Treatment and outcomes of clear cell sarcoma of the kidney: A report from the Children's Oncology Group studies AREN0321 and AREN03B2."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: DIRECT
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "When combining studies, the 4-year EFS rates for patients who had stage I (n = 10), II (n = 47), III (n = 65), and IV (n = 10) disease were 90% (95% CI, 73.2%-100.0%), 93.4% (95% CI, 86.4%-100.0%), 82.8% (95% CI, 74.1%-92.6%), and 58.3% (95% CI, 34%-100.0%), respectively."
    explanation: >-
      Pooling AREN0321 with the AREN03B2 patients who received AREN0321
      stage-appropriate chemotherapy, the COG report gives a four-year
      event-free survival of 90% (95% CI 73.2% to 100%) for the ten stage I
      patients. The figure is four-year where the NWTS-5 one is five-year, and
      it rests on ten patients.
  - reference: PMID:38396300
    reference_title: "Treatment and outcomes of clear cell sarcoma of the kidney: A report from the Children's Oncology Group studies AREN0321 and AREN03B2."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: DIRECT
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "In the Children's Oncology Group study AREN0321 of risk-adapted therapy, RT was omitted for stage I disease if lymph nodes were sampled, and carboplatin was added for stage IV disease (regimen UH-1). ... There were no local recurrences among seven patients with stage I disease who were treated without RT. One stage I recurrence occurred in the brain, which was the most common site of relapse overall."
    explanation: >-
      The COG report states the stage I change on AREN0321: radiotherapy was
      omitted for stage I disease when lymph nodes were sampled. None of the
      seven stage I patients treated without radiotherapy had a local
      recurrence, and the one stage I recurrence was in the brain, a distant
      site. The result shows no local failure after radiotherapy was left out
      in a small group, not an absence of relapse.
  - reference: PMID:38396300
    reference_title: "Treatment and outcomes of clear cell sarcoma of the kidney: A report from the Children's Oncology Group studies AREN0321 and AREN03B2."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: DIRECT
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "The seemingly superior outcomes for patients with stage I CCSK treated on the COG regimens suggests that stage I CCSK based on upfront nephrectomy is more favorable than stage I CCSK after neoadjuvant chemotherapy. This observation makes intuitive sense given that staging differences between COG and SIOP have resulted in COG selecting a very small and highly favorable subgroup of patients with CCSK for omission of RT. In the SIOP studies 39.5% (75/191) of patients with CCSK had stage I, compared to only 7.6% (10/132) in our study using COG staging. A patient who has a COG stage II or III tumor (if resected upfront) could have a SIOP stage I tumor after preoperative chemotherapy."
    explanation: >-
      The COG authors contrast stage I under the two systems. COG stage I rests
      on upfront nephrectomy and SIOP stage I follows neoadjuvant
      (preoperative) chemotherapy, so a tumour that would be COG stage II or III
      if resected upfront could be SIOP stage I. They cite 75 of 191 (39.5%)
      SIOP patients as stage I against 10 of 132 (7.6%) under COG staging, and
      suggest that stage I based on upfront nephrectomy is more favourable.
      This is the authors' reading of a cross-system comparison, not a tested
      difference.
  - reference: PMID:23880476
    reference_title: "Clear cell sarcomas of the kidney registered on International Society of Pediatric Oncology (SIOP) 93-01 and SIOP 2001 protocols: a report of the SIOP Renal Tumour Study Group."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: DIRECT
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "A total of 191 CCSK patients (64% male) were analysed, with a median age at diagnosis of 2.6 years. Stage distribution for stages I, II, III and IV was 42%, 23%, 28% and 7%, respectively. ... Radiotherapy was applied in 2/80 stage I, 33/44 stage II, 44/54 stage III and 6/13 stage IV patients."
    explanation: >-
      The SIOP Renal Tumour Study Group report on 191 patients of the SIOP
      93-01 and SIOP 2001 trials gives 42% as stage I, and its radiotherapy
      figures count 80 stage I patients, of whom 2 were irradiated. The 42%
      differs from the 39.5% (75 of 191) that the COG report cites for the same
      SIOP studies, and the abstract does not say why.
- name: Stage II
  description: >-
    On NWTS-5, 44 of the 108 staged patients had stage II disease, with
    five-year event-free and overall survival of 88% and 98%. NWTS-5 classified
    any invasion of the renal sinus vessels as stage II, where NWTS-4 had
    classified invasion within the hilar plane as stage I in the absence of
    other stage II disease, and NWTS-4 patients were reclassified by the NWTS-5
    criteria for comparison. On AREN0321 and AREN03B2, the 47 stage II patients
    had a pooled four-year event-free survival of 93.4%. Stage II and III
    patients on AREN0321 received regimen I with radiotherapy; there were no
    local recurrences among the 47 stage II patients, and the authors suggest
    that omitting radiotherapy for stage II disease could be investigated in a
    future study.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:30255545
    reference_title: "Impact of cyclophosphamide and etoposide on outcome of clear cell sarcoma of the kidney treated on the National Wilms Tumor Study-5 (NWTS-5)."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: DIRECT
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Stage distribution was as follows: stage I, 12; II, 44; III, 45; IV, 7. ... Five-year EFS for stage I-IV was 100%, 88%, 73%, and 29%, respectively."
    explanation: >-
      The NWTS-5 abstract gives 44 stage II patients among the trial's 108
      eligible patients (12, 44, 45 and 7 for stages I to IV) and a five-year
      event-free survival of 88% for stage II.
  - reference: PMID:30255545
    reference_title: "Impact of cyclophosphamide and etoposide on outcome of clear cell sarcoma of the kidney treated on the National Wilms Tumor Study-5 (NWTS-5)."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: DIRECT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Patients on NWTS-4 with invasion of the renal sinus vessels within the hilar plane (a line joining the most medial aspects of the upper and lower poles) were classified as Stage I (if there was no other evidence of Stage II disease). On NWTS-5, patients with any invasion of the renal sinus vessels were classified as Stage II. To compare outcome between NWTS-4 and NWTS-5, NWTS-4 patients were classified using NWTS-5-defined stage, and estimates of outcome were made."
    explanation: >-
      The methods of the NWTS-5 report state how the trial changed the stage
      II definition: any invasion of the renal sinus vessels was stage II on
      NWTS-5, whereas NWTS-4 had classified invasion within the hilar plane as
      stage I when there was no other evidence of stage II disease. NWTS-4
      patients were restaged by the NWTS-5 definition before the two trials
      were compared. The sentences describe the trial's staging procedure and
      give one criterion of the stage, not a full definition.
  - reference: PMID:30255545
    reference_title: "Impact of cyclophosphamide and etoposide on outcome of clear cell sarcoma of the kidney treated on the National Wilms Tumor Study-5 (NWTS-5)."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: DIRECT
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "prognostic factors were assessed by focusing only on the remaining 89 patients with stage II or III disease. The five-year EFS and OS for stage II patients were 88% (95% CI: 77%-99%) and 98% (95% CI: 92%-100%) and for stage III were 73% (95% CI: 57%-87%) and 89% (95% CI: 79%-99%). Prognostic factors examined included age, gender, race, stage, sinus vessel invasion, tumor spill, tumor capsule invasion, sinus soft tissue invasion and lymph node involvement. No factor was predictive of OS in the multivariate analysis. Only age equal to or greater than 12 months correlated with better EFS and OS by univariate analysis (P=0.0024) and was borderline significant in the multivariate analysis for EFS (P=0.046)."
    explanation: >-
      The NWTS-5 results give five-year event-free and overall survival of 88%
      (95% CI 77% to 99%) and 98% (95% CI 92% to 100%) for stage II. The
      prognostic factor analysis in the same passage pooled the 89 stage II and
      III patients, so it does not single out stage II.

    supports: SUPPORT

    explanation: >-
      Pooling AREN0321 with the AREN03B2 patients who received AREN0321

  - reference: PMID:38396300
    reference_title: "Treatment and outcomes of clear cell sarcoma of the kidney: A report from the Children's Oncology Group studies AREN0321 and AREN03B2."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: DIRECT
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Patients who had stage II/III disease received regimen I with RT. ... In addition to the lack of local relapses amongst stage I patients, patients with stage II CCSK had excellent EFS, with no local recurrences among 47 patients. All three local recurrences occurred in patients with local stage III disease. Omission of RT for patients with stage II CCSK could be investigated in a future study, particularly as the late effects of abdominal RT continue to become more clear."
    explanation: >-
      Stage II and III patients on AREN0321 received regimen I with
      radiotherapy. There were no local recurrences among the 47 stage II
      patients, and the authors propose that omitting radiotherapy for stage II
      disease could be investigated in a future study because of the late
      effects of abdominal radiotherapy. The proposal is a suggestion for
      future work, not a tested change.
- name: Stage III
  description: >-
    On NWTS-5, 45 of the 108 staged patients had stage III disease, with
    five-year event-free and overall survival of 73% and 89%. NWTS-5 assessed
    prognostic factors, tumour spill among them, in its stage II and III
    patients together: no factor predicted overall survival on multivariate
    analysis, and only age of 12 months or more correlated with better
    event-free and overall survival on univariate analysis. On AREN0321 and
    AREN03B2, the 65 stage III patients had a pooled four-year event-free
    survival of 82.8%. Stage II and III patients on AREN0321 received regimen I
    with radiotherapy, all three local recurrences occurred in local stage III
    disease, and among local stage III tumours neither the initial procedure
    type, margin status nor lymph node involvement was prognostic. In a
    multicentre cohort of 54 children treated in Shanghai following NWTS
    strategies and staged by the NWTS system, five-year event-free survival was
    71.1% for the 21 stage III patients. Across that cohort, inferior vena cava
    tumour thrombus (6 patients; event-free survival 41.7% against 87.4%
    without it) and tumour rupture before or during surgery (5 patients; 40%
    against 86.8%) were adverse factors on univariate analysis, and tumour
    rupture was the only independent adverse factor on multivariate analysis
    (HR 5.9, 95% CI 1.4 to 24.5). The authors attribute part of their stage III
    result to tumour rupture, with event-free survival of about 86% for stage
    III patients without it.

- name: Stage IV
  description: >-
    Stage IV patients in the cited series had distant metastases at diagnosis.
    On NWTS-5, 7 of the 108 staged patients had stage IV disease; the lungs were
    the commonest metastatic site (3 patients), followed by bone (2), five of
    the seven relapsed, and five-year event-free survival for stage IV was 29%.
    On AREN0321 and AREN03B2, 7.6% of the 132 patients analysed had metastases
    (bone 20%, liver 20%, lung only 30%, lung and bone together 30%). AREN0321
    added carboplatin for stage IV disease (regimen UH-1). The ten stage IV
    patients of the two studies combined had a four-year event-free survival of
    58.3%, and the authors judge that they appeared to benefit from the
    carboplatin-containing regimen, although their outcomes remained
    unsatisfactory; the comparison is with the earlier NWTS-5 result rather
    than a concurrent control. On the SIOP 93-01 and SIOP 2001 trials, stage IV
    disease was a significant adverse prognostic factor for event-free
    survival.

pathophysiology:
- name: BCOR Internal Tandem Duplication
  description: >-
    The recurrent lesion of CCSK: a somatic internal tandem duplication (ITD)
    clustering in the C terminus of BCOR, within the sequence encoding the PUFD
    domain, which is important for forming the variant polycomb repressive
    complex 1 and through which BCOR binds PCGF1 in that complex. BCOR can act
    as a transcriptional corepressor: it interacts selectively with the POZ
    domain of BCL-6 and can potentiate BCL-6 repression. Somatic ITDs were found
    in 23 of 27 paediatric CCSKs (85%) from two independent cohorts, identical in
    primary and relapsed tumour pairs and absent from adjacent normal kidney and
    blood, and mutant BCOR transcripts and proteins were markedly upregulated in
    ITD-positive tumours. In another series the ITD was found in all 20 CCSKs and
    in none of 193 other paediatric renal tumours, and these CCSKs expressed
    only an aberrant BCOR allele, which the authors read as a close correlation
    between the BCOR aberration and CCSK tumorigenesis. Expression profiling of
    patient tumours suggested that CCSK arises within a renal mesenchymal cell,
    a later study suggested a primitive mesodermal cell able to differentiate
    towards both nephrons and stroma, and the cell of origin is not established.
  biological_scale: MOLECULAR
  genetic_context:
    variant_origin: SOMATIC
    variant_type: duplication
  gene:
    preferred_term: BCOR
    term:
      id: hgnc:20893
      label: BCOR
  molecular_functions:
  - preferred_term: transcription corepressor activity
    term:
      id: GO:0003714
      label: transcription corepressor activity
  protein_complexes:
  - preferred_term: variant polycomb repressive complex 1.1 (BCOR complex)
    term:
      id: GO:0140261
      label: BCOR complex
  cell_types:
  - preferred_term: putative renal mesenchymal cell
    term:
      id: CL:0008019
      label: mesenchymal cell
  locations:
  - preferred_term: kidney
    term:
      id: UBERON:0002113
      label: kidney
  notes: >-
    functional_impact_category is not set and transcription corepressor activity
    carries no modifier: the cited sources place the duplication in the PUFD
    domain and show the mutant allele expressed and upregulated, but do not
    establish whether corepressor function is lost, retained or altered. The
    cell type is a hedged suggestion from expression profiling, not an
    established cell of origin. OLS4 searches of CL restricted to label and
    synonym fields on 28 Oct 2026 returned no CL class for renal mesenchymal
    cell or for kidney mesenchymal cell; the second query matched only the
    imported GO process GO:0072161 (mesenchymal cell differentiation involved in
    kidney development). The same search for metanephric mesenchyme returned
    CL:0000324 metanephric mesenchyme stem cell (is_a CL:0000134 mesenchymal
    stem cell; OLS4 shows no definition for it), which is not bound because
    neither source names that cell: PMID:16299227 proposes only a renal
    mesenchymal cell, and PMID:24488803 offers a primitive mesodermal cell as a
    possibility, places the CCSK phenotype in the window of differentiation
    between intermediate mesoderm and pretubular aggregates, and states that
    the cell of origin is unknown. The generic CL:0008019 mesenchymal cell is
    therefore bound, with putative renal mesenchymal cell as preferred_term.
  downstream:
  - target: Primary Renal Tumor Growth
    causal_link_type: INDIRECT_UNKNOWN_INTERMEDIATES
    description: >-
      The duplication is the recurrent lesion of CCSK. Its link to tumour
      formation rests on its presence in most tumours and on the tumours
      expressing only an aberrant BCOR allele, which the authors read as a close
      correlation with tumorigenesis; the intervening steps are not established
      in the cited sources.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:26573325
    reference_title: "Recurrent internal tandem duplications of BCOR in clear cell sarcoma of the kidney."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: DIRECT
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Here we report on the identification of somatic internal tandem duplications (ITDs) clustering in the C terminus of BCOR in 23 of 27 (85%) pediatric clear cell sarcomas of the kidney (CCSK) from two independent cohorts. We profile CCSK tumours using a combination of whole-exome, transcriptome and targeted sequencing. Identical ITD mutations are found in primary and relapsed tumour pairs but not in adjacent normal kidney or blood. Mutant BCOR transcripts and proteins are markedly upregulated in ITD-positive tumours."
    explanation: >-
      Roy et al. profiled paediatric clear cell sarcomas of the kidney from two
      independent cohorts by whole-exome, transcriptome and targeted sequencing.
      They report somatic BCOR internal tandem duplications (ITDs) clustering in
      the BCOR C terminus in 23 of 27 (85%) tumours, identical ITDs in primary
      and relapsed tumour pairs, no ITD in adjacent normal kidney or blood, and
      markedly upregulated mutant BCOR transcripts and proteins in ITD-positive
      tumours. The quoted abstract sentences state each part of the claim
      directly; the abstract does not say how many relapsed pairs or normal
      samples were tested.
  - reference: PMID:26098867
    reference_title: "Consistent in-frame internal tandem duplications of BCOR characterize clear cell sarcoma of the kidney."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: DIRECT
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "We identified internal tandem duplications in the BCOR gene (BCL6 corepressor) affecting the C terminus in 100% (20/20) of CCSK tumors but in none (0/193) of the other pediatric renal tumors. CCSK tumors expressed only an aberrant BCOR allele, indicating a close correlation between BCOR aberration and CCSK tumorigenesis."
    explanation: >-
      The authors report their own finding: in their series, internal tandem
      duplications in BCOR affecting the C terminus were present in all 20 CCSK
      tumours and in none of 193 other paediatric renal tumours, and the CCSK
      tumours expressed only an aberrant BCOR allele. They read this as a close
      correlation between the BCOR aberration and CCSK tumorigenesis. This
      supports the node as a recurrent lesion of CCSK which, in this series, was
      absent from all the other paediatric renal tumours tested. The 100% figure
      comes from a single 20-tumour series and is not a frequency for CCSK as a
      whole. The abstract does not say whether the duplications are somatic, and
      it reports a correlation, not a demonstrated causal mechanism.
  - reference: PMID:27000436
    reference_title: "Mutually exclusive BCOR internal tandem duplications and YWHAE-NUTM2 fusions in clear cell sarcoma of kidney: not the full story."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: DIRECT
    quote_role: BACKGROUND
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Internal tandem duplication within the BCOR gene sequence that encodes the PUFD domain, important in the formation of the non-canonical or variant polycomb repressor complex 1 (v-PRC1), was very recently described in 100% of 20 clear cell sarcomas of kidney (CCSKs)."
    explanation: >-
      Kenny et al. open the abstract of their clear cell sarcoma of kidney cohort
      study by restating earlier work: the BCOR internal tandem duplication lies
      within the gene sequence that encodes the PUFD domain, and that domain is
      important in forming the non-canonical (variant) polycomb repressor
      complex 1. The sentence states both points outright. Binding the node's
      protein complex to GO:0140261 BCOR complex is the curator's mapping of the
      sentence's variant polycomb repressor complex 1 onto the GO class whose
      definition names the corepressor BCOR among its subunits; it is not a
      claim the sentence makes. Kenny et al. restate both points rather than
      test them, and this item does not rely on the sentence's figure for the
      earlier series. The sentence does not say that BCOR acts as a
      transcriptional corepressor, and it does not say whether the duplication
      raises or lowers the activity of the complex.
  - reference: PMID:26573325
    reference_title: "Recurrent internal tandem duplications of BCOR in clear cell sarcoma of the kidney."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: INDIRECT
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "All of the BCOR ITDs identified in this study target the C-terminal PUFD domain through which BCOR physically interacts with PCGF1 (ref. 14) within a variant PCR1 (PRC1.1/BCOR complex)"
    explanation: >-
      Roy et al. report, from their analysis of tumours from two independent
      cohorts (their own sequencing and re-analysed TARGET consortium
      transcriptomes), that every BCOR internal tandem duplication identified in
      their study targets the C-terminal PUFD domain. Citing earlier work, they
      add that this is the domain through which BCOR physically interacts with
      PCGF1 within a variant PRC1, the PRC1.1/BCOR complex, which GO names the
      BCOR complex (GO:0140261). That the domain is important for forming the
      complex is one inference step from this, because it is the domain through
      which BCOR binds PCGF1 inside the complex. The sentence says where the
      duplications sit and what the domain binds; it does not say that BCOR acts
      as a transcriptional corepressor, or whether the duplication raises or
      lowers the activity of the complex. 'PCR1' is the source's own spelling of
      PRC1 and is kept as written.
  - reference: PMID:10898795
    reference_title: "BCoR, a novel corepressor involved in BCL-6 repression."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: DIRECT
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    evidence_source: IN_VITRO
    snippet: "BCoR can function as a corepressor when tethered to DNA and, when overexpressed, can potentiate BCL-6 repression. ... Strikingly, BCoR interacts selectively with the POZ domain of BCL-6 but not with eight other POZ proteins tested, including PLZF."
    explanation: >-
      Huynh et al. identified BCoR in cell-based reporter and protein-interaction
      studies as a corepressor: it can function as a corepressor when tethered to
      DNA, can potentiate repression by BCL-6 when overexpressed, and interacts
      selectively with the POZ domain of BCL-6. This is the basis for binding
      transcription corepressor activity (GO:0003714) to this node, with no
      modifier. The study describes normal BCOR function in cultured cells and
      says nothing about the CCSK duplication or its effect on that function.
  - reference: PMID:16299227
    reference_title: "Clear cell sarcoma of the kidney: up-regulation of neural markers with activation of the sonic hedgehog and Akt pathways."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: DIRECT
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "This study suggests that CCSKs arise within a renal mesenchymal cell that shows a wide variety of neural markers."
    explanation: >-
      The authors compared gene expression in patient CCSKs with Wilms tumours
      and fetal kidney, confirmed key proteins, and proposed that CCSK arises
      within a renal mesenchymal cell that expresses many neural markers. This
      is the basis for the putative renal mesenchymal cell annotation on this
      node (bound to CL:0008019 mesenchymal cell). The authors offer it only as a
      suggestion, and their abstract describes the pathogenesis of CCSK as an
      enigma, so the cell of origin is not established. The study came before
      the reports of BCOR internal tandem duplications in CCSK (PMID:26098867,
      PMID:26573325) and does not report on BCOR, so it supports the cell-type
      annotation only, not the duplication itself.
  - reference: PMID:24488803
    reference_title: "Clear cell sarcoma of the kidney demonstrates an embryonic signature indicative of a primitive nephrogenic origin."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: INDIRECT
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "As this marker discriminates the intermediate mesoderm from other mesodermal structures, our study could suggest that CCSK arises from a mesodermal cell type that retains the capacity to initiate differentiation towards both nephrons and stroma, but remains locked in a primitive state."
    explanation: >-
      A later study profiled patient CCSKs by SNP array, expression array and
      marker immunofluorescence. FOXD1 (stromal progenitors) and CITED1 (nephron
      progenitors) were both highly expressed, and OSR1, the intermediate
      mesoderm marker that 'this marker' in the quote refers to, was expressed
      at higher levels than in Wilms tumour or normal kidney. From this the
      authors suggest that CCSK arises from a primitive mesodermal cell able to
      differentiate towards both nephrons and stroma. Reading that as a renal
      mesenchymal cell takes one inference step: the paper's introduction places
      both progenitor lineages in the metanephric mesenchyme, while its closing
      text places the CCSK phenotype in the window of differentiation between
      intermediate mesoderm and pretubular aggregates. The authors present the
      origin as a possibility, the same abstract says the cell of origin of CCSK
      is unknown, and the paper states that its findings do not by themselves
      prove a nephrogenic origin. Like PMID:16299227, the study came before the
      BCOR internal tandem duplication reports and supports only the cell-type
      annotation.
- name: "YWHAE::NUTM2 Fusion"
  description: >-
    The alternative recurrent lesion: t(10;17)(q22;p13), with breakpoints mapped
    to 17p13.3 and 10q22.3 in an index tumour, rearranges YWHAE and FAM22 into an
    in-frame YWHAE-FAM22 fusion transcript joining exons 1-5 of YWHAE to exons
    2-7 of FAM22; the FAM22 members involved include FAM22B and FAM22E, now
    NUTM2B and NUTM2E. The transcript was detected in 6 of 50 further CCSKs
    tested (12% in that cohort). In a cohort of 159 CCSKs the BCOR internal
    tandem duplication and the YWHAE fusion were mutually exclusive, and 11.8%
    of tumours carried neither when tested by those assays. In a 39-patient
    cohort tested by PCR, FISH or Sanger sequencing, a YWHAE rearrangement (1
    tumour), BCOR ITD (34) and BCOR::CCNB3 fusion (1) were mutually exclusive,
    and 3 tumours had none of them detected.
  biological_scale: MOLECULAR
  genetic_context:
    variant_type: translocation

      id: hgnc:12851

    translocation in tumour tissue and do not report testing of constitutional
    DNA. FAM22B and FAM22E are the previous HGNC symbols of NUTM2B and NUTM2E.
    The cited sources do not characterise the molecular function of the fusion.
    The 39-patient cohort reports a YWHAE rearrangement without naming its
    partner gene, so counting that tumour under this node is an inference.

  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:22294382
    reference_title: "Characterization of the chromosomal translocation t(10;17)(q22;p13) in clear cell sarcoma of kidney."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: DIRECT
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Using fluorescently labelled bacterial artificial chromosomes (BACs) and a chromosome-walking strategy on an index case of CCSK with t(10;17)(q22;p13) by karyotype, we identified the chromosomal breakpoints on 17p13.3 and 10q22.3. The translocation results in rearrangement of YWHAE on chromosome 17 and FAM22 on chromosome 10, producing an in-frame fusion transcript of ~3 kb, incorporating exons 1-5 of YWHAE and exons 2-7 of FAM22, as determined by RT-PCR using YWHAE- and FAM22-specific primers. The YWHAE-FAM22 transcript was detected in six of 50 further CCSKs tested, therefore showing an overall incidence of 12% in our cohort. ... Based on the chromosome 10 breakpoint identified by FISH and the sequences of the full-length transcripts obtained, the FAM22 members involved in the translocation in these CCSK cases include FAM22B and FAM22E."
    explanation: >-
      O'Meara et al. used fluorescently labelled BACs and chromosome walking on
      an index CCSK with t(10;17)(q22;p13) to map the breakpoints to 17p13.3 and
      10q22.3. The translocation rearranges YWHAE on chromosome 17 and FAM22 on
      chromosome 10 into an in-frame YWHAE-FAM22 fusion transcript, joining
      exons 1 to 5 of YWHAE to exons 2 to 7 of FAM22 (structure determined by
      RT-PCR), and the transcript was detected in six of 50 further CCSKs
      tested, which the authors report as an overall incidence of 12% in their
      cohort. The FAM22 members involved in these cases include FAM22B and
      FAM22E, which HGNC records as previous symbols of NUTM2B (hgnc:23445, NUT
      family member 2B) and NUTM2E (hgnc:23448, NUT family member 2E).
      YWHAE-FAM22 is therefore the fusion this node calls YWHAE::NUTM2.
  - reference: PMID:27000436
    reference_title: "Mutually exclusive BCOR internal tandem duplications and YWHAE-NUTM2 fusions in clear cell sarcoma of kidney: not the full story."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: DIRECT
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "We analysed a cohort of 159 CCSKs and can now not only confirm that there is indeed mutual exclusivity of these BCOR and YWHAE mutations, but also show that a substantial proportion (in this series 11.8%) of CCSKs bear neither mutation when tested by these assays, raising the possibility of distinct aetiologies for subsets of CCSK."
    explanation: >-
      Kenny et al. tested 159 clear cell sarcomas of the kidney for the two
      lesions their abstract has just named, the BCOR internal tandem
      duplication and the YWHAE-NUTM2 transcript, and confirm that the two are
      mutually exclusive. In this series 11.8% of tumours carried neither when
      tested by these assays, which the authors read as raising the possibility
      of distinct aetiologies for subsets of CCSK. This is the largest cohort
      cited here for the exclusivity, and because the abstract names the fusion
      as YWHAE-NUTM2, placing its lesion under this node needs no inference. The
      abstract does not give the number of tumours carrying each lesion, and
      'neither' is relative to the assays used, as the sentence itself says.
  - reference: PMID:36563883
    reference_title: "Clinical relevance of BCOR internal tandem duplication and TP53 aberration in clear cell sarcoma of the kidney."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: INDIRECT
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "BCOR ITD, YWHAE rearrangement, BCOR-CCNB3 fusion and TP53 status were examined by polymerase chain reaction, fluorescence in situ hybridization, or Sanger sequencing in a cohort of 39 patients with CCSK. Among them, 34 cases (87.18%) had BCOR ITD, 1 (2.56%) had YWHAE rearrangement, and 1 (2.56%) had BCOR-CCNB3 gene fusion. The remaining 3 (7.69%) harbored none of these aberrations. BCOR ITD, YWHAE rearrangement and BCOR-CCNB3 were mutually exclusive."
    explanation: >-
      In a cohort of 39 CCSK patients tested by PCR, FISH or Sanger sequencing,
      34 tumours (87.18%) carried a BCOR internal tandem duplication, one
      (2.56%) a YWHAE rearrangement and one (2.56%) a BCOR-CCNB3 fusion. No
      tumour carried more than one of these lesions, and 3 (7.69%) had none of
      them detected. The mutual exclusivity of the YWHAE and BCOR-CCNB3 lesions
      rests on a single tumour each. The abstract calls the YWHAE lesion a
      rearrangement and does not name its partner gene, so reading it as the
      YWHAE::NUTM2 fusion is an inference.

- name: Distant Metastasis to Bone, Brain and Lung
  description: >-

      Of the 37 relapses in the SIOP and AIEOP cohort, 35 (95%) were metastatic,
      and the brain (13), lungs (7) and bone (5) were the commonest relapse
      sites, so relapsed CCSK is almost always distant spread, most often to
      these three organs. The full text counts these as relapses confined to
      that site, so they are not totals of all brain, lung or bone involvement,
      and the ranking describes relapse rather than metastasis at diagnosis.
      'Thirt-five' is the source's own spelling and is kept as written.

histopathology:
- name: Classic Pattern of Cords and Fibrovascular Septa
  finding_term:
    preferred_term: cords and nests of tumor cells separated by fibrovascular septa
    term:
      id: NCIT:C49203
      label: Fibrovascular Septa Present
  description: >-
    The classic histological pattern of CCSK is cords or nests of tumour cells
    separated by fibrovascular septa. The cells are described as epithelioid,
    or as round or spindle-shaped with clear cytoplasm, with round to ovoid
    nuclei and inconspicuous nucleoli. The septa are thin and regularly
    branching, ranging from a thin chicken-wire arrangement to broad sheets
    containing an arborizing capillary vasculature, and myxoid pools also
    separate the cords. The classic pattern is found at least focally in the
    majority of tumours.
  notes: >-
    NCIT has terms for parts of the classic pattern, NCIT:C53642 Cord-Like
    Pattern, NCIT:C35892 Nested Pattern and NCIT:C49203 Fibrovascular Septa
    Present. The septa are bound because both sources describe fibrovascular
    septa separating or surrounding the cords of tumour cells. NCIT:C47835
    Nesting (Zellballen) Growth Pattern is not bound: NCIT defines it as nests
    of tumor cells surrounded by a discontinuous layer of sustentacular cells
    and fibrovascular stroma, and neither source describes sustentacular
    cells. NCIT:C96347 Chicken-Wire Vasculature is not bound either: NCIT
    defines it as delicate vascular channel formations of arborizing and
    curving capillaries, which fits only the thin end of the range of septa
    that PMID:24488803 describes. NCIT:C4264 Clear Cell Sarcoma of the Kidney,
    this entry's NCIT mapping, names the tumour rather than a finding. The
    candidates came from a label search of the full OLS4 descendant listing
    of NCIT:C83490 Histopathology Result, retrieved through the OLS4 API on
    28 Oct 2026.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:30736802
    reference_title: "Expression of cyclin D1 in clear cell sarcoma of kidney. Is it useful in differentiating it from its histological mimics?"
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: DIRECT
    quote_role: BACKGROUND
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "On histological examination, classic CCSK is characterized by nests or cords of epithelioid cells with round to oval nuclei. Myxoid pools and thin and regularly branching fibrovascular septa separate the cords of tumor cells"
    explanation: >-
      In the introduction to their cyclin D1 study, Uddin et al. describe
      classic CCSK as nests or cords of epithelioid cells with round to oval
      nuclei, separated by myxoid pools and by thin, regularly branching
      fibrovascular septa. The sentences restate the published description,
      for which the authors cite earlier papers, rather than report the
      study's own cases.
  - reference: PMID:24488803
    reference_title: "Clear cell sarcoma of the kidney demonstrates an embryonic signature indicative of a primitive nephrogenic origin."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: DIRECT
    quote_role: BACKGROUND
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "The classic microscopic pattern of CCSK includes cords of round or spindle-shaped cells with clear cytoplasm and ovoid to rounded vesicular nuclei with inconspicuous nucleoli. The cells are surrounded by fibrovascular septa ranging from a thin \"chicken-wire\" like arrangement to broad sheets containing an arborizing capillary vasculature (Argani et al., 2000). This classic pattern is found at least focally in the majority of the tumors"
    explanation: >-
      The introduction of Karlsson et al.'s expression-profiling study
      describes the classic pattern as cords of round or spindle-shaped cells
      with clear cytoplasm and ovoid to rounded vesicular nuclei, surrounded by
      fibrovascular septa that range from a thin chicken-wire arrangement to
      broad sheets containing an arborizing capillary vasculature, and states
      that the pattern is found at least focally in the majority of tumours.
      It cites Argani et al. (2000), the first author and year of the National
      Wilms Tumor Study Group Pathology Center review cited elsewhere in this
      entry (PMID:10632483), whose abstract names the classic pattern without
      describing it. The sentences restate that description rather than
      report the study's own tumours, and they give no proportion behind the
      majority.
- name: Variant Histologic Patterns
  finding_term:
    preferred_term: multiple blended histologic patterns
    term:
      id: NCIT:C35867
      label: Morphologic Finding
  description: >-
    CCSK shows several histological patterns besides the classic one. A
    National Wilms Tumor Study Group Pathology Center review identified nine
    major patterns (classic, myxoid, sclerosing, cellular, epithelioid,
    palisading, spindle, storiform and anaplastic), and virtually all tumours
    contained multiple patterns that blended with one another. Citing that
    review, a later paper lists the components mixed with the classic pattern
    in descending order of frequency as myxoid, sclerosing, cellular,
    epithelioid, palisading, spindle-cell, storiform and anaplastic. A review
    notes that this diversity of patterns can mimic most other paediatric
    renal tumours and often leads to confusion and misdiagnosis.
  notes: >-
    NCIT has terms for several of the single patterns, for example
    NCIT:C35998 Myxoid Stroma Formation, NCIT:C49015 Nuclear Palisading and
    NCIT:C35888 Storiform Growth Pattern. Its terms for a mixture are not
    bound. NCIT defines NCIT:C201796 Heterogeneous Morphology as the presence
    of two or more distinct neoplastic cellular components in a tissue
    sample, and NCIT:C35907 Biphasic Pattern as the presence of two
    morphologically distinct cellular components, whereas the source
    describes patterns that blend with one another; NCIT:C41610 Mixed
    Cellular Population describes a cellular infiltrate. The row is therefore
    bound to NCIT:C35867 Morphologic Finding, which NCIT defines as a light
    microscopic finding that describes the cellular characteristics and
    architectural patterns of cell populations in a tissue sample. The
    candidates came from a label search of the full OLS4 descendant listings
    of NCIT:C83490 Histopathology Result and NCIT:C35867, retrieved through
    the OLS4 API on 28 Oct 2026.

    In the National Wilms Tumor Study Group Pathology Center review,
    immunohistochemical stains were performed on 45 cases and only vimentin

    including epithelioid tumours that mimicked Wilms tumour, and

- name: BCOR Immunoreactivity
  finding_term:
    preferred_term: BCOR immunoreactivity
    term:
      id: NCIT:C40998
      label: Immunophenotypic Finding
  description: >-
    A review states that immunohistochemistry for BCOR has been shown to be a
    sensitive and specific marker for CCSK in the context of paediatric renal
    tumours. In one series, strong diffuse nuclear BCOR immunoreactivity was
    seen in 15 of 18 CCSKs (83%) and in 2 of 10 Wilms tumours, and none of
    the malignant rhabdoid tumours or congenital mesoblastic nephromas showed
    any immunoreactivity, so the lack of strong diffuse staining does not
    exclude CCSK. Strong BCOR staining is not confined to CCSK: five renal or
    perirenal sarcomas in patients aged 30 to 62 years, which raised the
    differential diagnosis of CCSK because of their morphology and strong
    BCOR immunoreactivity, proved on RNA sequencing to be malignant solitary
    fibrous tumours.
  notes: >-
    The full OLS4 descendant listing of NCIT:C40998 Immunophenotypic Finding,
    retrieved through the OLS4 API on 28 Oct 2026, includes no BCOR term, and
    OLS4 searches of NCIT for BCOR Positive and BCOR Expression returned BCOR
    genetic-alteration, fusion and tumour terms and the BCORL1 gene (for
    example NCIT:C178467 BCOR-CCNB3 Fusion Protein Expression) but no BCOR
    immunostaining result. The row is therefore bound to the branch term,
    NCIT:C40998 Immunophenotypic Finding.

  - reference: PMID:26573325
    reference_title: "Recurrent internal tandem duplications of BCOR in clear cell sarcoma of the kidney."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: DIRECT
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Here we report on the identification of somatic internal tandem duplications (ITDs) clustering in the C terminus of BCOR in 23 of 27 (85%) pediatric clear cell sarcomas of the kidney (CCSK) from two independent cohorts. We profile CCSK tumours using a combination of whole-exome, transcriptome and targeted sequencing.     explanation: >-
      Roy et al. profiled paediatric clear cell sarcomas of the kidney from two
      independent cohorts by whole-exome, transcriptome and targeted sequencing.
      They report somatic BCOR internal tandem duplications (ITDs) clustering in
      the BCOR C terminus in 23 of 27 (85%) tumours, identical ITDs in primary
      and relapsed tumour pairs, no ITD in adjacent normal kidney or blood, and
      markedly upregulated mutant BCOR transcripts and proteins in ITD-positive
      tumours. The quoted abstract sentences state each part of the claim
      directly; the abstract does not say how many relapsed pairs or normal
      samples were tested.
  - reference: PMID:36563883
    reference_title: "Clinical relevance of BCOR internal tandem duplication and TP53 aberration in clear cell sarcoma of the kidney."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: DIRECT
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "BCOR ITD, YWHAE rearrangement, BCOR-CCNB3 fusion and TP53 status were examined by polymerase chain reaction, fluorescence in situ hybridization, or Sanger sequencing in a cohort of 39 patients with CCSK. Among them, 34 cases (87.18%) had BCOR ITD, 1 (2.56%) had YWHAE rearrangement, and 1 (2.56%) had BCOR-CCNB3 gene fusion. The remaining 3 (7.69%) harbored none of these aberrations. BCOR ITD, YWHAE rearrangement and BCOR-CCNB3 were mutually exclusive."
    explanation: >-
      In a cohort of 39 CCSK patients tested by PCR, FISH or Sanger sequencing,
      34 tumours (87.18%) carried a BCOR internal tandem duplication, one
      (2.56%) a YWHAE rearrangement and one (2.56%) a BCOR-CCNB3 fusion. No
      tumour carried more than one of these lesions, and 3 (7.69%) had none of
      them detected. The mutual exclusivity of the YWHAE and BCOR-CCNB3 lesions
      rests on a single tumour each.
differential_diagnoses:
- name: Wilms Tumor
  disease_term:
    preferred_term: Wilms tumor
    term:
      id: MONDO:0006058
      label: Wilms tumor
  description: >-
    A 1985 series concluded that, until CCSK is shown to be related to
    nephroblastoma, identifying it as a non-Wilms sarcoma seems appropriate.
    BCOR internal tandem duplication and cyclin D1 immunostaining help
    distinguish CCSK from blastemal Wilms tumour, but neither test is absolute.
    In a cohort of 159 CCSKs, 11.8% carried neither the ITD nor the YWHAE
    fusion, so a negative ITD test does not exclude CCSK. Strong diffuse cyclin
    D1 staining was seen in only 11 of 18 CCSKs in one tissue-microarray
    series, and in another the epithelial component of most Wilms tumours
    stained diffusely and often strongly while the blastema was negative in
    most, so the cyclin D1 contrast holds for Wilms tumour blastema.
  distinguishing_features:
  - BCOR internal tandem duplication, found in most but not all CCSKs, and in none of 193 other paediatric renal tumours tested in one series and none of 18 Wilms tumours in another
  - Diffuse strong cyclin D1 immunostaining in most CCSKs (all 14 cases in one series, 7 of 7 assessable cases in a second, 11 of 18 in a third), against only rare positive nuclei in the blastema of most Wilms tumours in the first series and negative blastema in 18 Wilms tumours in the second; in a fourth series the epithelial component of most Wilms tumours stained, so the contrast applies to blastema
  - Cytokeratin-negative immunostaining in all CCSKs of one series, including epithelioid tumours that mimicked Wilms tumour, where consistently negative stains helped exclude other tumours in the differential diagnosis
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:26573325
    reference_title: "Recurrent internal tandem duplications of BCOR in clear cell sarcoma of the kidney."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: DIRECT
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "BCOR ITDs were not found in a cohort of other childhood renal tumours (18 Wilms tumours and 9 congenital mesoblastic nephromas) and soft-tissue sarcomas (n=10)."
    explanation: >-
      In the study that found BCOR internal tandem duplications in most clear
      cell sarcomas of the kidney, the duplications were not found in a cohort of
      other childhood renal tumours that included 18 Wilms tumours and 9
      congenital mesoblastic nephromas, nor in 10 soft-tissue sarcomas. This
      directly supports BCOR ITD as a feature that separates CCSK from Wilms
      tumour.
  - reference: PMID:26098867
    reference_title: "Consistent in-frame internal tandem duplications of BCOR characterize clear cell sarcoma of the kidney."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: INDIRECT
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "We identified internal tandem duplications in the BCOR gene (BCL6 corepressor) affecting the C terminus in 100% (20/20) of CCSK tumors but in none (0/193) of the other pediatric renal tumors."
    explanation: >-
      In this series, BCOR internal tandem duplications were found in all 20 CCSK
      tumours and in none of the 193 other paediatric renal tumours tested, so
      the lesion separated CCSK from every other paediatric renal tumour
      examined. The abstract does not say which tumour types made up the 193 or
      how many were Wilms tumours, so its bearing on the Wilms tumour
      differential rests on the premise that Wilms tumours were among them.
  - reference: PMID:4065884
    reference_title: "Clear cell sarcoma of the kidney: a clinicopathologic study of 21 patients with long-term follow-up evaluation."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: INDIRECT
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Until substantial proof is found that clear cell sarcoma of the kidney is related to nephroblastoma, its identification as a non-Wilms' sarcoma would seem to be appropriate."
    explanation: >-
      The authors conclude that, until clear cell sarcoma of the kidney is shown
      to be related to nephroblastoma, identifying it as a non-Wilms sarcoma
      seems appropriate. This supports treating Wilms tumour as a separate
      disease from CCSK. That the two must be told apart in diagnosis follows
      only by inference, from the relation between them that the sentence leaves
      open. The sentence describes no clinical, imaging or histological feature
      that distinguishes the two tumours, so it supports no distinguishing
      feature in this row.
  - reference: PMID:25556515
    reference_title: "Diffuse and strong cyclin D1 immunoreactivity in clear cell sarcoma of the kidney."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: DIRECT
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Cyclin D1 expression was evaluated in 59 renal tumours-CCSK (14), WT (25), rhabdoid tumour (four), Ewing sarcoma (five), and CMN (11)-and four neuroblastomas. All 14 CCSKs showed diffuse and strong reactivity. In contrast, the blastematous component of most WTs showed only rare positive nuclei, that of rhabdoid tumours showed rare to focal immunoreactivity, and that of more than half of CMNs showed weak or focal immunoreactivity. Most Ewing sarcomas and all neuroblastomas showed diffuse moderate to strong staining. ... Cyclin D1 is most helpful in distinguishing CCSK from WT, rhabdoid tumour, and some CMNs, but not from neuroblastoma or Ewing sarcomas."
    explanation: >-
      Mirkovic et al. stained the following for cyclin D1: 14 CCSKs, 25 Wilms
      tumours and four rhabdoid tumours (all among 59 renal tumours that also
      included Ewing sarcomas and CMNs), plus four neuroblastomas. Every CCSK
      stained diffusely and strongly. The blastema of most Wilms tumours showed
      only rare positive nuclei, and the rhabdoid tumours showed rare to focal
      staining, whereas all four neuroblastomas stained diffusely (moderate to
      strong). The authors conclude that cyclin D1 helps distinguish CCSK from
      Wilms tumour and rhabdoid tumour but not from neuroblastoma. The rhabdoid
      and neuroblastoma comparisons rest on four tumours each.
  - reference: PMID:25751590
    reference_title: "Novel Karyotypes and Cyclin D1 Immunoreactivity in Clear Cell Sarcoma of the Kidney."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: DIRECT
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Excluding a case with poor tissue section antigenicity, 7 of 7 CCSKs (100%) showed diffuse and strong nuclear cyclin D1 staining. Cyclin D1 immunohistochemistry was also performed on tissue microarrays of other pediatric renal tumors: blastemal areas of 18 WT cases were negative; 6 rhabdoid tumors and 1 metanephric adenoma showed patchy and weak staining; 3 mesoblastic nephromas and 18 of 29 neuroblastomas had positive staining. Cyclin D1 immunohistochemistry helps distinguish CCSK from blastemal WT and metanephric adenoma and rhabdoid tumors, but not from neuroblastomas and mesoblastic nephromas."
    explanation: >-
      A second series, by a different group, shows the same direction for Wilms
      tumour, rhabdoid tumour and neuroblastoma. The two series differ on
      mesoblastic nephroma: this one reports positive staining in 3 mesoblastic
      nephromas and concludes that cyclin D1 does not separate them from CCSK,
      whereas the first found it helpful for some. Cyclin D1 was diffuse and
      strong in 7 of 7 assessable CCSKs (one case was excluded for poor
      antigenicity). On tissue microarrays of
      other paediatric renal tumours, the blastemal areas of 18 Wilms tumours
      were negative and 6 rhabdoid tumours showed patchy and weak staining,
      while 18 of 29 neuroblastomas stained. The authors conclude that cyclin D1
      helps distinguish CCSK from blastemal Wilms tumour and rhabdoid tumours
      but not from neuroblastomas. Neuroblastoma staining was frequent but not
      universal in this series, and the comparator tumours were stained on
      tissue microarray cores rather than whole sections.

  - reference: PMID:30736802
    reference_title: "Expression of cyclin D1 in clear cell sarcoma of kidney. Is it useful in differentiating it from its histological mimics?"
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: DIRECT
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT

    explanation: >-
      Uddin et al. stained 19 CCSKs, 9 Wilms tumours and other paediatric

  - reference: PMID:27000436
    reference_title: "Mutually exclusive BCOR internal tandem duplications and YWHAE-NUTM2 fusions in clear cell sarcoma of kidney: not the full story."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: DIRECT
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "We analysed a cohort of 159 CCSKs and can now not only confirm that there is indeed mutual exclusivity of these BCOR and YWHAE mutations, but also show that a substantial proportion (in this series 11.8%) of CCSKs bear neither mutation when tested by these assays, raising the possibility of distinct aetiologies for subsets of CCSK."
    explanation: >-
      In a cohort of 159 CCSKs, 11.8% carried neither the BCOR internal tandem

      test for it does not on its own exclude CCSK when Wilms tumour is the
      alternative.

    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT

    snippet: "Cyclin D1 expression was evaluated in 59 renal tumours-CCSK (14), WT (25), rhabdoid tumour (four), Ewing sarcoma (five), and CMN (11)-and four neuroblastomas. All 14 CCSKs showed diffuse and strong reactivity. In contrast, the blastematous component of most WTs showed only rare positive nuclei, that of rhabdoid tumours showed rare to focal immunoreactivity, and that of more than half of CMNs showed weak or focal immunoreactivity. Most Ewing sarcomas and all neuroblastomas showed diffuse moderate to strong staining. ... Cyclin D1 is most helpful in distinguishing CCSK from WT, rhabdoid tumour, and some CMNs, but not from neuroblastoma or Ewing sarcomas."
    explanation: >-
      Mirkovic et al. stained the following for cyclin D1: 14 CCSKs, 25 Wilms
      tumours and four rhabdoid tumours (all among 59 renal tumours that also
      included Ewing sarcomas and CMNs), plus four neuroblastomas. Every CCSK
      stained diffusely and strongly. The blastema of most Wilms tumours showed
      only rare positive nuclei, and the rhabdoid tumours showed rare to focal
      staining, whereas all four neuroblastomas stained diffusely (moderate to
      strong). The authors conclude that cyclin D1 helps distinguish CCSK from
      Wilms tumour and rhabdoid tumour but not from neuroblastoma. The rhabdoid
      and neuroblastoma comparisons rest on four tumours each.
  - reference: PMID:25751590
    reference_title: "Novel Karyotypes and Cyclin D1 Immunoreactivity in Clear Cell Sarcoma of the Kidney."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: DIRECT
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Excluding a case with poor tissue section antigenicity, 7 of 7 CCSKs (100%) showed diffuse and strong nuclear cyclin D1 staining. Cyclin D1 immunohistochemistry was also performed on tissue microarrays of other pediatric renal tumors: blastemal areas of 18 WT cases were negative; 6 rhabdoid tumors and 1 metanephric adenoma showed patchy and weak staining; 3 mesoblastic nephromas and 18 of 29 neuroblastomas had positive staining. Cyclin D1 immunohistochemistry helps distinguish CCSK from blastemal WT and metanephric adenoma and rhabdoid tumors, but not from neuroblastomas and mesoblastic nephromas."
    explanation: >-
      A second series, by a different group, shows the same direction for Wilms
      tumour, rhabdoid tumour and neuroblastoma. The two series differ on
      mesoblastic nephroma: this one reports positive staining in 3 mesoblastic
      nephromas and concludes that cyclin D1 does not separate them from CCSK,
      whereas the first found it helpful for some. Cyclin D1 was diffuse and
      strong in 7 of 7 assessable CCSKs (one case was excluded for poor
      antigenicity). On tissue microarrays of
      other paediatric renal tumours, the blastemal areas of 18 Wilms tumours
      were negative and 6 rhabdoid tumours showed patchy and weak staining,
      while 18 of 29 neuroblastomas stained. The authors conclude that cyclin D1
      helps distinguish CCSK from blastemal Wilms tumour and rhabdoid tumours
      but not from neuroblastomas. Neuroblastoma staining was frequent but not
      universal in this series, and the comparator tumours were stained on
      tissue microarray cores rather than whole sections.

treatments:
- name: Radical Nephrectomy
  description: >-
    Surgical removal of the tumour-bearing kidney. In a Shanghai multicentre
    cohort treated following NWTS strategies, treatment comprised upfront
    radical nephrectomy followed by radiotherapy and intensive chemotherapy, and
    the authors note that upfront surgery gives a definite pathological
    diagnosis. On the SIOP 93-01 and SIOP 2001 trials, 169 of 191 CCSK patients
    received chemotherapy before nephrectomy and all underwent total
    nephrectomy.
  treatment_term:
    preferred_term: radical nephrectomy
    term:
      id: NCIT:C51778
      label: Radical Nephrectomy
  therapeutic_modality: SURGERY
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:38980838
    reference_title: "Outcomes of children with clear cell sarcoma of kidney following NWTS strategies in Shanghai China (2003-2021)."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: DIRECT
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Treatment comprised upfront radical nephrectomy, followed by radiotherapy and intensive chemotherapy. ... Upfront surgery of NWTS strategies can make a definite pathology diagnosis, but how to reduce tumor rupture during surgery is important especially in developing countries."
    explanation: >-
      Zhang et al. studied children with CCSK treated following NWTS strategies
      at children's hospitals in Shanghai. Treatment comprised upfront radical
      nephrectomy, followed by radiotherapy and intensive chemotherapy. In their
      conclusions the authors say the upfront surgery of NWTS strategies gives a
      definite pathological diagnosis, and that reducing tumour rupture during
      surgery is important, especially in developing countries. The sentences
      summarise this cohort's NWTS-based protocol, not a cooperative-group
      protocol, so they support the claim for this cohort rather than for every
      patient treated on NWTS strategies.
  - reference: PMID:23880476
    reference_title: "Clear cell sarcomas of the kidney registered on International Society of Pediatric Oncology (SIOP) 93-01 and SIOP 2001 protocols: a report of the SIOP Renal Tumour Study Group."

Pre-operative chemotherapy was administered to 169/191 patients. All patients underwent total nephrectomy and 189/191 patients received post-operative chemotherapy."
    explanation: >-
      The SIOP Renal Tumour Study Group analysed prospectively collected data on
      CCSK patients entered onto the SIOP 93-01 and SIOP 2001 trials.
      Pre-operative chemotherapy was given to 169 of 191 patients, all underwent
      total nephrectomy, and 189 of 191 received post-operative chemotherapy, so
      on these SIOP protocols most CCSK patients had chemotherapy before
      surgery. The report says nothing about NWTS, and it calls the operation
      total nephrectomy rather than radical nephrectomy, so it supports the
      timing contrast but not the specific radical procedure.
- name: Regimen I Chemotherapy
  description: >-
    On the single-arm National Wilms Tumor Study-5, the 108 eligible patients
    received vincristine/doxorubicin/cyclophosphamide alternating with
    cyclophosphamide/etoposide for 24 weeks (regimen I) with radiation to sites
    of disease, and had five-year event-free survival of 79% and overall
    survival of 90%. Five-year event-free survival for stages I to IV was 100%,
    88%, 73% and 29%, from groups of 12, 44, 45 and 7 patients, so the stage I
    and stage IV figures rest on small numbers. The outcome was similar to the
    earlier NWTS-4 regimen but reached over a shorter treatment duration, and
    the authors concluded that regimen I showed more benefit for stage I and II
    disease than for higher stages, where new therapies are needed.

    description: >-
      Comparison of the single-arm NWTS-5 trial with the earlier NWTS-4 trial,
      with NWTS-4 patients reclassified by NWTS-5 staging criteria. The paper
      reports no formal test of a stage-by-treatment interaction, so the stage
      gradient is a cross-trial observation rather than a tested subgroup
      effect. For higher-stage disease the authors found no apparent benefit of
      regimen I over the NWTS-4 regimen.

For higher stage disease, the benefit of regimen I is not apparent as compared to the three drugs utilized in NWTS-4."
      explanation: >-
        The NWTS-5 report states the stage-dependent effect: patients with stage
        I and II disease treated with regimen I had better disease control than
        NWTS-4 patients treated with three drugs over 15 months, while for
        higher-stage disease the benefit of regimen I was not apparent. The
        comparison is with a separate, earlier trial whose patients were
        reclassified by NWTS-5 staging criteria, not a randomised comparison.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:30255545
    reference_title: "Impact of cyclophosphamide and etoposide on outcome of clear cell sarcoma of the kidney treated on the National Wilms Tumor Study-5 (NWTS-5)."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: DIRECT
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Treatment consisted of vincristine/doxorubicin/cyclophosphamide alternating with cyclophosphamide/etoposide for 24 weeks and radiation to sites of disease. ... One hundred eight eligible patients were enrolled on study (69% males, 63% Caucasian), with a median age of 22 months. Stage distribution was as follows: stage I, 12; II, 44; III, 45; IV, 7. ... Five-year EFS and OS were 79% (95% CI: 71%-88%) and 90% (95% CI: 84%-96%). Five-year EFS for stage I-IV was 100%, 88%, 73%, and 29%, respectively. ... The outcome for patients with CCSK treated on NWTS-5 was similar to NWTS-4 and accomplished over a shorter treatment duration. Stage was highly predictive of outcome. Brain metastases occurred more frequently than on NWTS-4. Regimen I showed more benefit for patients with stage I and II disease as compared with higher stages of disease where new therapies are needed."
    explanation: >-
      Abstract of the NWTS-5 report on its own prospective single-arm trial in
      clear cell sarcoma of the kidney. Treatment was
      vincristine/doxorubicin/cyclophosphamide alternating with
      cyclophosphamide/etoposide for 24 weeks, with radiation to sites of
      disease; the full-text introduction calls this four-drug chemotherapy
      regimen I. Of the 108 eligible patients, 12, 44, 45 and 7 had stage I to
      IV disease. Five-year EFS was 79% and OS 90%, and five-year EFS for stages
      I to IV was 100%, 88%, 73% and 29%, so the stage I and stage IV figures
      come from groups of 12 and 7 patients. The authors judge the overall
      outcome similar to NWTS-4 but reached over a shorter treatment duration,
      note that brain metastases were more frequent than on NWTS-4, and conclude
      that regimen I showed more benefit for stage I and II disease than for
      higher stages, where new therapies are needed. That benefit comes from
      comparison with the earlier NWTS-4 trial, not from a randomised
      comparison; the full text attributes it to the chemotherapy because
      patients on both trials received tumour-bed radiation, and finds no
      benefit at higher stages. Every NWTS-5 patient also received radiation, so
      the survival figures belong to the combined-modality protocol, not to
      regimen I alone.
- name: Carboplatin-Containing Chemotherapy for Stage IV Disease
  description: >-
    The Children's Oncology Group study AREN0321 added carboplatin for stage IV
    disease (regimen UH-1). Only four stage IV patients were treated on
    AREN0321, which the authors judge too few to determine whether regimen UH-1
    improved outcomes compared with NWTS-5. Pooled with patients enrolled only
    on the AREN03B2 biology study who received the same stage-appropriate
    chemotherapy, the ten stage IV patients had a four-year event-free survival
    of 58.3%, against a five-year event-free survival of 29% on NWTS-5, and on
    this basis stage IV patients appeared to benefit from the
    carboplatin-containing regimen, although their outcomes remained
    unsatisfactory. The comparison is with earlier NWTS-5 results, not a
    concurrent control arm.

  - reference: PMID:38396300

    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "When combining studies, the 4-year EFS rates for patients who had stage I (n = 10), II (n = 47), III (n = 65), and IV (n = 10) disease were 90% (95% CI, 73.2%-100.0%), 93.4% (95% CI, 86.4%-100.0%), 82.8% (95% CI, 74.1%-92.6%), and 58.3% (95% CI, 34%-100.0%), respectively. ... Patients with metastatic CCSK at diagnosis remain rare, with just four patients treated on AREN0321, a similar proportion as prior studies. ...     explanation: >-
      The results give ten stage IV patients across the pooled AREN0321 and
      AREN03B2 cohorts, with a four-year EFS of 58.3% (95% CI 34% to 100%). The
      discussion states that only four stage IV patients were treated on
      AREN0321, too few to determine whether regimen UH-1 improved outcomes
      compared with NWTS-5, and that the pooled outcome suggests chemotherapy
      intensification may be beneficial when set against the five-year EFS of
      29% on NWTS-5. The apparent benefit therefore rests on ten patients and on
      a comparison of a four-year pooled figure with a five-year historical one.
- name: Radiation Therapy
  description: >-
    Radiotherapy to sites of disease accompanied regimen I on the National Wilms
    Tumor Study-5, where every patient received postoperative radiation to the
    tumour bed (10 Gy) and to other infradiaphragmatic and metastatic sites as
    necessary, whatever the completeness of resection. The Children's Oncology
    Group study AREN0321 omitted radiotherapy for stage I disease when lymph
    nodes were sampled; none of the seven stage I patients treated without it
    had a local recurrence, and one stage I recurrence occurred in the brain.
    The authors relate their better stage I outcomes, compared with SIOP's, to
    staging: with nephrectomy first, COG staging selects a very small and highly
    favourable stage I group for omission of radiotherapy, 7.6% of their
    patients against 39.5% stage I in the SIOP studies. The single-arm NWTS-5
    trial cannot separate the contribution of radiotherapy from that of the
    chemotherapy.
  treatment_term:
    preferred_term: radiation therapy
    term:
      id: NCIT:C15313
      label: Radiation Therapy
  therapeutic_modality: RADIOTHERAPY
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:30255545
    reference_title: "Impact of cyclophosphamide and etoposide on outcome of clear cell sarcoma of the kidney treated on the National Wilms Tumor Study-5 (NWTS-5)."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: INDIRECT
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Treatment consisted of vincristine/doxorubicin/cyclophosphamide alternating with cyclophosphamide/etoposide for 24 weeks and radiation to sites of disease. ... Five-year EFS and OS were 79% (95% CI: 71%-88%) and 90% (95% CI: 84%-96%). Five-year EFS for stage I-IV was 100%, 88%, 73%, and 29%, respectively. ... All patients received postoperative radiation therapy to the tumor bed (10 Gy) and other infradiaphragmatic and metastatic sites as necessary. ... Radiation therapy was delivered to all sites of disease regardless of the completeness of surgical resection."
    explanation: >-
      From the NWTS-5 report on its own prospective single-arm trial. The
      abstract gives the protocol, vincristine/doxorubicin/cyclophosphamide
      alternating with cyclophosphamide/etoposide for 24 weeks with radiation to
      sites of disease, and its outcome: five-year EFS 79%, OS 90%, and
      five-year EFS for stages I to IV of 100%, 88%, 73% and 29%. The full-text
      methods and discussion describe the radiation: postoperative radiation to
      the tumour bed (10 Gy) and to other infradiaphragmatic and metastatic
      sites as necessary, given to all sites of disease however completely the
      tumour was resected. This directly establishes radiotherapy as part of
      first-line CCSK therapy on NWTS-5, but supports its effect on outcome only
      indirectly, because every patient also received the chemotherapy and
      there was no unirradiated group. The authors attribute the stage I and II
      improvement over NWTS-4 to the chemotherapy, because patients on both
      trials received tumour-bed radiation, and state that the role of radiation
      therapy in this cancer needs to be further elucidated.
  - reference: PMID:38396300
    reference_title: "Treatment and outcomes of clear cell sarcoma of the kidney: A report from the Children's Oncology Group studies AREN0321 and AREN03B2."
    supports: SUPPORT

One stage I recurrence occurred in the brain, which was the most common site of relapse overall."
    explanation: >-
      The COG report on AREN0321 states that radiotherapy was omitted for stage
      I disease when lymph nodes were sampled. None of the seven stage I
      patients treated without radiotherapy had a local recurrence. The one
      stage I recurrence was in the brain, a distant site that the authors judge
      local radiotherapy would likely not have prevented, so the result shows no
      local failure after leaving out radiotherapy, not an absence of relapse.
      The authors note that the stage I group is small.
  - reference: PMID:38396300
    reference_title: "Treatment and outcomes of clear cell sarcoma of the kidney: A report from the Children's Oncology Group studies AREN0321 and AREN03B2."
    supports: SUPPORT
    directness: DIRECT
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "The seemingly superior outcomes for patients with stage I CCSK treated on the COG regimens suggests that stage I CCSK based on upfront nephrectomy is more favorable than stage I CCSK after neoadjuvant chemotherapy. This observation makes intuitive sense given that staging differences between COG and SIOP have resulted in COG selecting a very small and highly favorable subgroup of patients with CCSK for omission of RT. In the SIOP studies 39.5% (75/191) of patients with CCSK had stage I, compared to only 7.6% (10/132) in our study using COG staging."
    explanation: >-
      The COG authors compare their stage I results with SIOP's and suggest
      that stage I CCSK staged on upfront nephrectomy is more favourable than
      stage I CCSK staged after neoadjuvant chemotherapy. COG staging selected a
      very small and highly favourable subgroup for omission of radiotherapy:
      10 of 132 patients (7.6%) were stage I, against 75 of 191 (39.5%) in the
      SIOP studies. The absence of local recurrence after omitting
      radiotherapy therefore comes from a small, favourable group selected by
      COG staging.
- name: Salvage Chemotherapy for Relapsed Disease
  description: >-
    Chemotherapy for relapse after complete remission. In a combined SIOP and
    AIEOP series, 30 of 37 relapsed patients received chemotherapy in different
    combinations, 19 had surgical excision and 18 radiotherapy, and 14 went on
    to high-dose chemotherapy with autologous bone marrow transplantation; 22
    of the 37 reached a second complete remission, 15 of whom relapsed again.
    Five of the 13 patients with an isolated brain relapse received ICE
    (ifosfamide, carboplatin and etoposide), and three of them achieved a
    long-term complete response, which the authors read as possibly promising
    for brain relapse while noting that they have no data on the influence of
    upfront treatment; one patient died of septic shock after one cycle of
    ICE. The agents and regimen bound here are those of ICE, one of the
    combinations used. No standard treatment guideline was available for
    relapsed CCSK except in the United Kingdom, and the authors state that too
    few relapsed patients have been reported to establish the contributions of
    chemotherapy, high-dose chemotherapy with stem-cell transplantation,
    radiotherapy and surgery, so no standard treatment guidelines are available
    for this patient group.
  treatment_term:
    preferred_term: salvage chemotherapy

      id: NCIT:C15632
